<page-num>124</page-num>

# COUNSEL ON MARRIAGE
<meta>The title refers to the alchemical treatise "Consilium Conjugii," which discusses the "marriage" or chemical union of opposites like Sol and Luna.</meta>

<margin>,</margin>although it stands in contrast to the **greater world** <note>original: *maiorem mundum*; the Macrocosm or the entire universe</note>, because our **egg** <note>original: *ouum*; a metaphor for the hermetically sealed vessel and the matter inside it</note> is said to be "higher" in its center—specifically because of the **Sun**—and "lower" at its extremities, that is, the other two parts. This is because the **egg of the world** <note>the universe conceived as a giant egg</note> is called "higher" at its poles and "lower" at its center. In the middle of our egg is the point of the Sun, like a chick in an egg, and according to this, it is considered superior.

In the egg of the world, however, the opposite is true: namely, its center is "lower." For example: in the world, **fire** is the most subtle, lightest, and highest **element** <term>elementum: one of the four traditional building blocks of matter (earth, air, fire, water)</term>, but in that [alchemical] egg, fire is the lowest element because it is situated in the middle. Conversely, in the world, **earth** is the thickest, heaviest, and lowest element, but in the egg—namely, the outer shells—the shells are the earth and are the "higher" element; and so it goes for the others.

Therefore, it is true that the egg conducts itself in a manner contrary to the egg of the world. Regarding the egg of the world, the opposite holds because the world-egg is called "higher" at its poles and "lower" at its center, or according to its extremes—that is, **Saturn** and the **Moon**, which in the world are respectively "lower" and "higher." **Saturn** is more superior and elevated and 

<summary>This page explains the "Doctrine of the Egg," arguing that the alchemical vessel (the Microcosm) functions as an inverted mirror of the universe (the Macrocosm), where the "solar" center is the most noble part.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical egg, Macrocosm, Microcosm, elements, fire, earth, Saturn, Sun, four elements, cosmological inversion</keywords>

--- Page 137 ---
<meta>This page continues the comparison between the alchemical "egg" (the vessel) and the cosmic order. It emphasizes that the alchemical process must rely on its own internal "moist" fire—which mimics the life-giving heat of the Sun—rather than a harsh, external flame.</meta>

<page-num>125</page-num>

# OF THE SUN AND MOON.

The Moon is lower [than the Sun], as are the other six **Planets**. It follows: Behold, within the **egg** there are four, namely **earth**, **water**, **air**, and **fire**, and in the middle is a red point of the **Sun**, which is the **chick** <note>original: *pullus*; the developing "embryo" of the Philosopher's Stone</note>. This is the reason why the **Philosophers** have named this art "the egg."

It follows. A saying of **Assiduus** <note>an alchemical authority cited in medieval texts</note>: It does not work by an alien fire or by an external spirit, because, as **Senior** <note>Senior Zadith, a 10th-century Arabian alchemist</note> says, "Do not allow anything alien to enter into it, for it would corrupt it and destroy the whole." Or, put more clearly: it does not work by an alien fire, but by its own fire, which is **moist**. For external fire is uneven and excessive, lacking temperance <note>or balance</note>.

The entire efficacy of this work depends on its own fire. As **Morienes** <note>a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist</note> and **Geber** <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan, an influential 8th-century chemist</note> say: "Only Solar heat generates a good **temperament**" <term>complexionem: the specific balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, wet, and dry—within a substance</term>, which the heat of fire (that is, nature) imitates.

It follows: Since when that "other thing" is digested—that is, the **water of life** <term>aqua vitae: "water of life," a potent alchemical solvent or distillate</term> of Akis <note>likely a reference to a specific alchemical substance or preparation</note>, which is an **animated tincture** <note>original: *animata tinctura*; a substance capable of "dyeing" or transforming matter</note>, incorruptible—and its form is perfected—namely, after it has been **whitened** <note>original: *dealbatum*; the stage of the work known as the Albedo</note>—it is then ready for the operation. Therefore, it should not be taken up before the **Melancholy**, that is, the **blackness**— <note>original: *nigredo*; the first stage of the work, associated with "Melancholy" or the decay of the material</note>

<summary>This page explains the metaphor of the alchemical "egg," identifying the four elements and the central "Sun" (the chick) within the vessel. It stresses the importance of using a gentle, internal "moist" fire rather than harsh external heat to achieve a balanced transformation.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical egg, internal fire, four elements, Sun and Moon, Senior Zadith, Geber, Morienes, water of life, nigredo, albedo</keywords>

### Explanation of the letter of the Sun to the waxing Moon in the solar year, which begins:

"In her thinness or her tenderness"—that is, in her extreme novelty, namely, of her marriage with the **Sun** <note>Sol</note>, which is to say, her illumination by him. The Sun says to her: "I will give you light from my beauty," namely solar light, by which the perfection of the **tincture** <term>tinctura: the transformative essence or "dye" believed to change the color and quality of metals</term> is reached; for it is exalted...

...solar light. For everything "high" <note>original: *altum*</note>—that is, every **sublimated spirit** <term>spiritus sublimatus: a substance purified and raised through heat into a gaseous state</term>—is [brought] from you into our "water of the sea"; or rather, everything high—that is, every Planet—is perfected through him. He <note>the author</note> places the "efficient" <note>the cause or active agent</note> for the "effect." For as **Morienes** <note>Morienus, a legendary hermit-alchemist said to have taught the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid</note> says: "The root of this science is in the hour of its composition, and you must wait day and night for its **fixation**" <term>fixio: the process by which a volatile substance is made stable and resistant to heat</term>. Therefore, let all haste be driven far away from the craftsman himself; instead, foresight, reason, and firm expectation should be applied. For this reason, a **gentle fire** <term>ignis blandus: a low, steady heat, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen or the sun</term> ought to be applied for a longer time, which should always last equally throughout its days, lest the body be consumed by hasty combustion. It follows: "Since you are the Sun, perfect in all parts of light..."

[He who] wishes to be hurried, by his vehemence ruins the work; because it is not the work of the craftsman, but the work of nature with the artist serving and helping her. *It follows:* "The lamp of light," namely the *Solar* [light], shall be poured into my lamp, that is, the lunar one, from you, namely the *Sun*, and from me, the *Moon*, there shall be an infusion of luminous light, just as the mixing of wine with sweet water; so from me and you comes the mixing of lights,

you will be fought henceforth, like copper and lead.
It follows: *The Lion, that is the Sun in the lower realm, is debased by the flesh.*
This is a metaphor; just as the strong Lion and king of animals is debased by his
weak flesh—for from the fourth day to the fourth, he naturally suffers a 
quartan fever <note>original: "quartanam"; a fever recurring every four days. In medieval alchemy, this "sickness" of the metal represents a state of impurity or the beginning of a transformative breakdown</note>. 
Thus the Lion, by nature, is debased and Eclipsed through his 
flesh, which is of a Lunar nature tempered to his own.

->### OF THE SUN AND MOON.<-

...[restricting] and congealing by its coldness and dryness, the very nature within the mass; **Jupiter**, however, by its heat and moisture, digests it. **Mars**, through its heat and dryness, gathers together things of the same kind <note>original: "homogenea"</note> and separates those that are different <note>original: "hethe-rogenea"</note>. **Venus**, indeed, through her coldness and moisture, arranges the limbs and the image. In this way, **natural Alchemy** <term>Alchemy: here referring to the natural process of metallic growth within the earth, which the alchemist seeks to replicate</term> is born through natural principles and by the impression of a form—specifically a natural, substantial form—while the craftsman serves as an assistant.

For **bronze** <note>original: "aes"; often used by alchemists to refer to base metal or the "body" of the work</note> does not possess anything of itself except **accidental forms** <note>In Aristotelian philosophy, "accidental" forms are outward traits like color or shape, whereas "substantial" forms are the core essence of what a thing is</note>, but it assists nature; by arranging the principles, it introduces **substantial forms**. 

And the place of generation, although it may be artificial, nevertheless imitates the natural one, because it is hollow and enclosed. Within this space, the smoke of the natural principles—generated by nature by means of a long-lasting and continuous heat—is reflected back upon itself for a long time. Once it is mixed in the form of a vapor, it congeals into a **metal**, the vapor of which corresponds to the species that forms its root. <margin>}</margin> 

From these statements, the solution to two arguments against the possibility of this art is clear. One of these is that art does not have the power to induce...

The second point is that the **natural place** must be the principle of generation. Some argue, however, stating that metals are generated according to the influence of the **Planets**, and they claim this celestial alignment is hidden from **Alchemists**. They believe it would be necessary for the chemist to begin their work at the exact moment when celestial nature begins to move the mineral in the earth.

To these critics, it must be answered that the **movers of the heavens**, whenever and wherever they find matter properly prepared and possessing the potential for such a form, begin at that moment to act through their **impressions** <term>Impressions: the "stamping" of celestial qualities or virtues onto physical matter</term> toward that form—provided that the craftsman joins the **active** principles with the **passive** ones. 

For it is said that the **Sun** and **man** generate a man <note>original: "Sol enim & homo generant hominem." This is a famous medieval philosophical principle derived from Aristotle, meaning that human life requires both biological parents and the life-giving energy of the heavens.</note>, yet it is not necessary for the man fathering a child to have prior knowledge of the Sun’s specific impressions; it is sufficient that the active and passive principles are brought together when the matter is ready. 

The **Sun** disposes and moves matter, just as the other **Planets** do, according to its local distance from the celestial sphere; thus, while it might be possible to begin the work at any time, one time is nevertheless more suitable than another. For **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary sage and mythical founder of alchemy</note> says in the second book of the *Seven Treatises*: "That this ele—"

[The increase] is moist in the spring and summer; it is necessary, therefore, that it be taken between this and that—namely, fifteen days after the entry of the **Sun** into **Aries**, until its entry into **Taurus**. 

For in this time, it will be increased further, and it is necessary to distill this stone while the **Sun** is in **Aries** and in **Taurus**; these things <term>Hermes: Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the hermetic tradition and alchemy</term> has said. Therefore, each **Planet** imprints its own image and impression individually, just as it does upon a human fetus, until it is perfected.

For as **Morienus** <note>a legendary hermit-alchemist said to have taught the King Khalid ibn Yazid</note> says to the King: "Know that earth, or stone, or egg, or blood, or vinegar, or any such things like **sulfur**, **quicksilver**, or **orpiment** <note>a yellow arsenic mineral often used as a pigment</note> are false; they are useless for this <term>mastery: the "magisterium" or the successful completion of the Great Work</term>, and they lead to a bad end." Men often grind and cook all these things, drawing extracts from their boiling, and then burying what they have extracted; in their own minds, they believe they are doing well. And so that their error is further reinforced—and lest they lose hope—they are unable to find what they seek, yet they continue hoping to find it. 

And regarding the writing of false books, **Senior** <note>referring to Senior Zadith, an influential authority in medieval alchemy</note> says: "For such a man composed his book, thinking he understood that hidden mystery, yet he himself neither knows nor understands a single letter of what he wrote in his book, being far removed from the true books of the Philosophers." And if he is questioned about these matters, he answers from those same books using metaphors; and if he is challenged with proof, he defends himself with quibbling—

...from uncertain things, they have removed from themselves the noteworthy things—that is, the things to be desired—namely the "station of knowledge" (which is stable knowledge) and its source <note>original: "mineram"; literally "ore" or "mine," but used here to mean the origin or foundation of the science</note>. The matter is manifest, yet it is treated by a wandering mind and an unstable intellect [fixated] on wandering, unfixed, and manifold things, even though the truth of the Art exists without diversity. There is among us, namely the **Philosophers** <term>Philosophos</term>, a difference—that is, a diversity—in what they think, specifically what they think concerning the art. And the preparation of the ignorant, though they continue the art, annihilates the person occupied in it; he neither knows, nor is there any success in the art, nor a key, nor locks (the problems of the wise). Neither does he know that the **coagulating agent** sends forth the **tincture** <term>tincturam</term> <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance or spirit capable of penetrating and transforming the "body" of a base metal into gold.</note>, nor does he arrive at a successful result. of life** <note>original: "aquæ vitæ"; in alchemy, this refers to a purified solvent or spiritual essence, not simply alcohol.</note>, which is the **tincture** <term>tinctura: the transformative essence or "dye" capable of changing the properties of matter</term>—and the power of the inferior—
that is, the **earth**—are together of a similar nature,
because they are generated and corrupted by the same 
generation. Their corruption, however, is their **putrefaction** <term>putrefactio: a stage where the matter decays and turns black, seen as a necessary death before a spiritual rebirth</term>, which
disposes the whole of generating nature for the better.
Because, as **Morienes** <note>original: "Morienes ad regem"; referring to Morienus Romanus, a legendary hermit who taught the Arabic prince Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> says to the king,
the entire strength of this **mastery** <term>magisterium: the "Great Work" or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term>
does not exist except after putrefaction; for
nothing was ever animated or brought forth
by birth except after putrefaction.
For if it has not been putrefied, it will not be
fused, nor will it be dissolved; and if it has not been dissolved,
it will return to nothing. It follows:
**Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of the alchemical tradition.</note> said, the crown is made from the 
residue—that is, the remains of this **divine water**—
which does not overlook the "thing"—that is, the

meaning **blackness** <term>nigredo: the "blackening" or "darkness" stage of the work, representing the chaos of the prime matter</term>. Secondly, in the second work,

what he says: the "West"—that is, the **rain-like moisture** <note>original: "humor pluuialis"</note>—
brings it to life—namely the earth—and

-># ADVICE ON THE [ALCHEMICAL] MARRIAGE<-

The way of the Philosophers is a lock, and a lock fitted within a lock <note>original: "sera & sera infra seram"</note>; that is, an obscurity obscured by obscurity. This is because the type and figure are hidden within a trope <term>trope: a metaphorical or figurative use of a word or expression</term>, which is its key—and one of them, because there is but one way, one arrangement, and one action. Then, in his Poem at the end, he places the "first weight." Know, my son, that the Roots are no fewer than three: that is, the third half-Moon <note>Luna</note> with that certain Sun <note>Sol</note>; toward that thing—that is, the aqueous moisture—this is projected 

<margin>They concealed nothing except the preparation <note>original: "Nihil occultaverunt nisi praeparatione"</note></margin>
by "ignoring"—that is, by concealing—what is within that stone <term>lapis: the Philosopher's Stone</term> during the works, because they have concealed nothing except its preparation.

For this reason, "ignoring" is not to be taken in an active sense, because the master is bound to know the operation; rather, it is taken passively. That is: I do not make it known to others who are unworthy, for God, the protector of man, knows into whom He wishes to inspire [this knowledge], and from whom He wishes to conceal it. Therefore, compare the symbolic figures to the explanation, for we have no image other than the picture of the Tablet of **Senior** <note>Senior Zadith (Ibn Umail), a 10th-century Arabic alchemist whose "Tabula Chimica" contained influential symbolic illustrations.</note>. One possessing consideration and discernment will see this more clearly than the radiant Sun, provided they approach the dispute with justice and not with injustice. Note what **Morienus** <note>Morienus Romanus, a legendary hermit-alchemist said to have taught the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid.</note> says to the King in his Dialogue:

If the wise ones who followed the ancient Philosophers and Prophets had not discovered their own explanations regarding the quality of the vessel <term>vas: the alchemical container or "Philosophical Egg"</term> in which they were to prepare the work, those who came after could never have achieved the perfection of this mastery. <margin>On the nature of the vessel</margin> Therefore, concerning the quality of the vessel, I commend this to your memory. <margin>1. Metallic 2. Glass 3. Earthenware</margin> **Gratianus** <note>Likely referring to an alchemical authority, though the name was common; many medieval texts attributed alchemical wisdom to earlier scholars to gain authority.</note> says: In the first stage, metallic vessels are useful. In the second stage, glass ones; and in the third, earthenware. That is to say: in the first "work," let the vessel be metallic; for the stage of **coagulation** <term>coagulation: the process of thickening or solidifying a liquid substance</term>, let it be glass; and for **fixation** <term>fixation: the process by which a volatile substance is rendered stable and resistant to fire</term>, let it be earthenware. <margin>A spherical shape like a half-moon at the bottom <image-desc>A small pen sketch of a semi-circular vessel base or crucible.</image-desc></margin> Furthermore, let the shape of all of them be spherical at the bottom, like a half-Moon, and as high as the quantity of the material requires. Some of the ancients, however, completed the work entirely in glass vessels, but such glass is not available to us, unless similar types can be found <note>The author likely refers to "Philosophical Glass," a legendary material capable of withstanding extreme heat without breaking.</note>. Certain other ancients finished the entire work in an earthenware vessel, such as **Zeno**. For the vessel must be made of a "millstone-like" rock <note>original: "lapideum molare"</note>, strong, non-porous, and well-fired, of the sort that glassmakers use for the crucibles <margin>furnace mortars <note>original Italian: "mortari delle fornaci"</note></margin> in which they melt their glass. Indeed, this vessel which lasts them for six weeks will easily last for six years in the Art, because the Art does not employ excessive heat,

-># THE COUNSEL OF MARRIAGE<-
<page-num>174</page-num>

However, it contains a spiritual clod <note>original: *glebam*; in alchemy, "gleba" or clod refers to a specific type of earth or raw mineral matter</note>, 
<margin>potter’s clay</margin> which does not melt in the fire like the common clod of the potters’ glue <note>original: *gluti*; likely referring to the binding quality of wet clay</note>, but resists it, being of a strong composition and 
<margin>lead</margin> non-porous. Nor can the vessel of the art be made of any other stone or lead, because any other stone would not remain whole in the fire but would be split, with a few exceptions. Therefore, it ought to be made from baked stone <note>likely referring to a refractory or fire-hardened material</note>. This is how such a vessel is prepared: Let the aforementioned clod be taken and dried, then let it be ground down, casting out stones, wood, foreign earth, and whatever is of another nature within them. Then, let almost half the part be burned in the fire so that it turns to stone <note>This describes the process of "calcination" or creating "grog"—pre-fired clay that is added to raw clay to reduce shrinkage and cracking</note>; grind this finely and sift it. 
<margin>glaze</margin> From this ground material, fashion a clod in place of [raw] earth, and mix it well so that it is sufficiently tenacious. From this, make the vessel and let it be baked in the fire. Allow it to be glazed <term>deuitreari: the process of applying a glass-like coating or glaze to ceramic</term> inside with a strong glass <note>original: *uitro forti*</note> that will not crack. It must not be glazed with lead glass, but with ash glass <note>Potash glass, made from plant ashes, which has a higher melting point and better durability than lead-based glazes</note>. Let a lid be made for it of the same kind, without a vent-hole. For it is a vessel that is neither a cucurbit <term>cucurbita: a gourd-shaped vessel used for boiling or distilling</term>, nor an alembic <term>alembic: the apparatus used for distilling, specifically the cap that redirects vapors</term>. Truly, it is a vessel made from two stones of one kind...

...[sulfu]r <note>original: *ris*; likely the ending of the word *sulfuris* (sulfur) or *corporis* (body) from the preceding page</note>, as Khalid <note>original: *Kalid*; referring to Khalid ibn Yazid, a legendary Umayyad prince often cited as one of the first great alchemists</note> the son of Isis says: that no metal is suitable for our vessel except clear, well-digested <term>digested: in alchemy, "digestion" refers to the process of applying gentle, consistent heat to a substance over a long period to mature it</term> iron, for no other would endure. For our water is not mixed with iron; wherefore Rhazes <note>original: *Raſis*; Abu Bakr al-Razi, a famous Persian polymath and alchemist</note> says in the *Book of Lights*: "If it be exalted in iron, it brings health." And he said before that a more efficient instrument cannot be devised, saying: "The most powerful administration for distilling, however, is this." And among other things, he requires a more efficient iron vessel, made of the clearest material, and in this it surpasses glass because it cannot be broken. And for certain, the first work <note>The "first work" usually refers to the initial stage of the Great Work, such as the *nigredo* or blackening</note> would be finished quickly in this vessel, because above the fire of a lamp, the putrefaction <term>putrefactio: the stage of the "Great Work" where the material is broken down, often associated with a black color</term> and the dissolution of the body <note>original: *corporis solutio*</note> would be swiftly completed. And as it is said among the experts, only one vessel is required. But among those still searching, many are prepared, and they are prepared individually; one is tested and examined in the next vessel. But then note that they ought to be of the same thickness, and as much of the material should be in one as in the other; let it be positioned so that the fire acts equally upon them, and let there be an equal exposure.

...positioning in the furnace, so that they extend four fingers or a little more [above the heat] and hang evenly inside, so that the light of the fire serves them equally. If, however, it is a glass vessel, let it be placed as evenly as the other, using the previously mentioned clay and mud.

<margin>The furnace should be 3 fingers thick.</margin>
You should make the furnace from the common clay of potters, prepared in the usual way, to a thickness of three fingers, a height of one standard cubit <note>original: *ulnæ capitalis*; a measurement roughly the length of a forearm</note>, and the width of a good palm, so that—

<margin>common fire all around</margin>
—the flame of the fire does not touch the bottom of the vessel, and so that it carries heat freely and equally to all parts.

<margin>air holes</margin>
It should also have air holes <term>spiracula: small vents or "breathing holes" used to control the airflow and temperature within the furnace</term> of three or four openings. Let the furnace be elevated evenly, and at the top, place a wheel upon it,

<margin>A wheel at the top, 2 fingers thick</margin>
made of good clay to a thickness of two fingers or a little more; this wheel should have one or two holes in the middle. And note that the whole [of the substance] should not be put into the preparation at once—as is the practice among experts—but rather it should be tested in part, so that no error occurs in the whole; or [use] two [supports] upon which your vessels may hang. And it is a good precaution that you do not increase the degrees of fire by double the previous amount, as Nodanus <note>Likely referring to an alchemical authority, though the name may be a corruption of a better-known author such as Morienus or Rhazes</note> teaches, [concerning]

### The Rule of Fire, and
### The Proportion of the REBIS, and
### Time.

For by a **slow fire** you will not err, but by excess you will ruin the work, even though it is cooked for a much longer time with a slow heat than with a sharp one. Note also with which parts of vinegar the **Rebis** <term>Rebis: the "double-thing" or "two-fold matter," a late-stage alchemical substance representing the union of opposites, such as spirit and body</term> is more quickly dissolved into water, but more slowly dried out and fixed <term>fixed: rendered non-volatile or solid so that it can withstand the heat of the fire without evaporating</term>. And this is the common method and teaching of the **Philosophers** <note>the traditional term for alchemists and natural scientists of the past</note>. Experts, however, have poured water over it so that it stood three fingers above, according to what 
<margin><unclear>faded scribal mark</unclear></margin>
seemed good to them. If, however, seven parts are taken, or perhaps fewer, the body of the copper <note>original: *æris*; a term frequently used for copper or its alloys in alchemical transformations</note> is dissolved more slowly. For nine parts act more strongly than six or seven, but a smaller amount is dried and fixed more quickly than a larger one. And whatever power can achieve in one operation, a double power will achieve in a short time according to the **Philosopher** <note>referring to Aristotle, the primary authority on natural physics</note>, provided there is an equal comparison of the proportion of the **agent to the patient** <term>agent/patient: the active substance (agent) that causes a change and the passive substance (patient) that receives the change</term>. ### OF THE SUN AND MOON.

<margin>Copper is burned; that is, it is whitened.</margin>
Divide the water into two parts; with 
one of these parts, the **copper** <term>copper: *aes* in Latin; in this context, it refers to the "body" or base metallic substance being transformed</term> must be burned. For that copper, when placed into that water, 
<margin>Ferment of gold</margin>
is called the **ferment of gold** <term>ferment: a substance that initiates a transformation in the "body" of the metal, much like yeast in dough</term>. Then the vessel must **imbibe** <note>soak up or absorb</note> the remaining water seven times until it drinks all the water; then, when all the moisture is dried out, let it be turned into dry earth. 
Next, let them be placed in a lit fire for 40 days, until they **putrefy** <term>putrefaction: the *Nigredo* or "blackness" stage where the material decomposes and turns black before changing colors</term>, and its colors appear. **Bonellus** <note>a recurring philosophical character in alchemical literature, likely a corruption of a Greek or Arabic name</note> says the same: Divide the water into two parts; with one part burn the body—that is, **whiten** it—according to **Lucas** in the *Turba* <note>the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers," a famous early alchemical text</note>, who says: to burn is to whiten. 
With the other part, however, cause it to putrefy. Know that the whole **regimen** <note>the systematic method or process of the work</note> does not happen except in water. 
Take, therefore, **our copper** <note>original: *es nostrum*; refers to the "Philosophical Copper," which practitioners claimed was different from common metal</note>, and place it in the vessel with its first part, and cook it for 41 days; cleanse it from all impurity, and cook it until its days are finished, and the **stone** <note>the Philosopher's Stone in its developmental stage</note> will become lacking in moisture. 
Next, cook it until nothing remains but the **dregs** <term>dregs: *fex* in Latin; the solid residue or "leavings" at the bottom of the vessel</term>. Then wash it seven times with clean water; when this is finished, let it putrefy in its own vessel until the intended goal appears to you as desired, and take care lest from the waters themselves...

# ADVICE ON THE MARRIAGE

<unclear>In this chapter</unclear>

For when the heat comes to it, by God's will, they will become water. Therefore, when you see that **blackness** <note>the *Nigredo* stage, symbolizing death and the stripping away of old forms</note> in the mineral water, know that the body has now been liquefied; then place it in its own vessel, and cook it for 40 days until it drinks the humors of **vinegar and honey** <note>symbolic terms for sharp/acidic and sweet/binding substances used in the process</note>. And some uncover the vessel every 7 days, or after 10 nights have passed, during which it appears as pure water, until the completion of 40 days. For then it drinks the **decoction of love** <note>original: *amoris decoctionem*; likely referring to the harmonious union of the elements</note>. Wash it again, and strip it of its blackness until it becomes earth, and the moisture perishes, and the stone remains dry to the touch.

Likewise, **Ephistus** <note>another authority from alchemical tradition</note> says: I command that the **whitening** <note>the *Albedo* stage, following the blackness</note> does not happen except by the cooking of the **Ethelia** <term>Ethelia: a term from the *Turba Philosophorum* referring to a volatile spirit, vapor, or the "hidden" part of the substance</term>, by grinding and soaking the **copper** <term>copper: *aes* in Latin; here representing the base material or "body" of the work</term>, treating it very frequently; however, do not pour the water on all at once, lest the **Elixir** <term>Elixir: written as *Ixir* in the text, from the Arabic *al-iksīr*, the transformative substance</term> be drowned. Therefore, when the moisture has dried and turned to powder, leave it for 40 days until the **colors** <margin>...colors</margin> described by the Philosophers appear.

-># OF THE SUN AND THE MOON<-
<note>original: *Solis et Lunae*; in alchemy, these are the standard symbols for Gold and Silver, or the masculine and feminine principles.</note>

And **Pandophilus** <note>an alchemical authority frequently cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*</note> commands one to cook until the whole becomes black, which **blackness** <note>the *Nigredo* stage, representing the death or putrefaction of the base matter</note> lasts for 40 days; then he teaches one to soak it in **fixed alum** <term>alumen fixum: a stabilized mineral salt used in alchemy as a purifying agent or to "fix" volatile substances</term> and **saffron** <note>original: *croco*; likely referring to a metallic oxide like *Crocus Martis* (iron oxide) rather than the culinary spice</note> for 40 days, until a third part is consumed. Likewise, **Athanius** commands 42 days, and again from the tenth day of September until the tenth day of **Libra** <note>the astrological sign of the Scales, indicating an autumn timeframe or a state of perfect balance in the vessel</note>, he says the second work is performed.

**Senior** <note>referring to Senior Zadith, the Latinized name of the 10th-century alchemist Ibn Umail</note>, however, desires that the **whitening** <note>original: *albedo*; the second major stage of the Great Work, signifying purification and spiritual washing</note> should begin to return after 150 days, and **Rhazes** <note>original: *Rasis*; the famous Persian physician and alchemist Abu Bakr al-Razi</note> says the same. **Morienes** <note>a legendary 7th-century hermit known for teaching the secrets of the stone to the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid</note>, however, first teaches that a complete **whitening** <note>original: *dealbationem*</note> is achieved in 21 days, saying: "O King, if you have seen the fixed white within it, even if you had sold your entire kingdom and your people, you could not compensate for the value of this work."

**Rhazes**, in his *Book of Lights*, says regarding the **governance** <term>regimen: the systematic management of heat and conditions during the alchemical process</term> of the work: "Thus finally it is fitting to roast and to moisten it alternately, so that it neither flees the fire nor emits smoke." And a little later he hands down the governance thus, saying that the **inhumation** <note>original: *inhumationem*; literally 'burial,' referring to placing the vessel in a warm, dark place like a dung-pit or sand bath to simulate the gestation of a seed in the earth</note> is completed in 21 continuous days; wherefore, if one spends 21 days on this, it will be necessary to devote the same amount of time both before and after. As **Rhazes** says in the *Book of Lights*: "It seems supremely necessary for the artisan to scrutinize, to estimate, and to love, nor in these matters should any [expense]..."

<meta>catchword: ...expense (spendij)</meta>

<summary>This page collates the opinions of various alchemical masters regarding the duration and "regimen" required to transition the matter from the black stage to the precious white stage. It emphasizes that while timelines differ (ranging from 21 to 150 days), the resulting "fixed white" is of incomparable value.</summary>

# COUNSEL OF CONJUNCTION
<note>original: *Consilium Conjugii*; a title referring to the alchemical "marriage" or union of opposites, such as spirit and body or sulfur and mercury.</note>

<margin>
Putrefaction in the vessel
</margin>
you should cause [the vessel] to be prepared, lest that which is in the vessel be corrupted and perish. And before all these things, near the beginning of this chapter, **Bonellus** <note>an alchemical authority cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*</note> says: Cook everything with a slow fire until all things that are in the vessel become water. For by the heat coming upon it, by God’s will, they shall become water. Seeing therefore that **blackness** <note>the *Nigredo* stage, indicating the breakdown or "death" of the material</note> in the **mineral water** <note>original: *aqua minerali*; likely a corrosive solvent or mercurial liquid</note>, know that the body has been liquefied now; then place it in its own vessel, and cook it for 40 days until it drinks the humors of **vinegar and honey** <note>metaphorical terms for acidic and sweet/sticky reagents used to nurture the growing "stone"</note>. And some uncover it every 7 days, or after 10 nights have passed, in which it appears to be pure water, until the completion of 40 days. For then it drinks the **decoction of love** <note>original: *amoris decoctionem*; likely a reference to the "marriage" or union of the substances in this specific text</note>. Wash it again, and deprive it of its blackness until it becomes earth, and the moisture perishes, and the stone remains dry to the touch.

Likewise, **Ephistus** <note>another figure from the *Turba* tradition</note> says: I command that the **whitening** <note>original: *dealbatio*; the Albedo stage of purification</note> does not happen except by the **decoction of Ethelia** <term>Ethelia: an alchemical term for the "vapors" or the hidden spirit/soul of the substance that rises during heating</term>, by grinding and soaking the **copper** <note>original: *æs*; used here as a code for the base matter or "body" of the work</note>, handling it very frequently; but do not pour the water all at once, lest the **elixir** <term>lxir: a variant of *elixir*, the transformative medicine or substance sought by alchemists</term> be submerged. Therefore, when the moisture has dried up and it is turned into powder, leave it for 40 days until the colors described by the **Philosophers** <note>the traditional name for alchemical masters</note> appear.

# OF THE SUN AND MOON
<note>original: *Solis et Lunae*; in alchemy, the Sun and Moon represent Gold and Silver, or the masculine and feminine principles (Sulfur and Mercury).</note>

And **Pandophilus** <note>a philosopher cited in the *Turba Philosophorum*, an early Latin alchemical text</note> orders [the practitioner] to cook, until the whole becomes **black**, which blackness lasts for 40 days; then he teaches to **imbibe** <note>to saturate or soak the material in a liquid</note> it in **fixed alum** <note>a stabilized mineral salt</note> and **saffron** <note>original: *croco*; likely refers to a reddish-gold oxide or "Crocus of Mars/Venus"</note> for 40 days, until a third part is consumed. Likewise, **Athanius** orders 42 days, and again from the tenth day of September until the tenth day of **Libra** <note>the zodiac sign following Virgo; this suggests a duration of roughly one month through the autumn equinox</note>, he says the second work is done. **Senior** <note>referring to Senior Zadith (Ibn Umayl), a 10th-century Arabic alchemist</note> indeed intends that the **whiteness** <note>the *Albedo* stage, representing purification</note> begins to turn after 150 days, and **Rhazes** says the same.

**Morienes** <note>a legendary hermit-alchemist said to have taught the art to King Khalid</note> truly teaches that the complete **whitening** is first done in 21 days, saying: "O King, if you saw the **fixed white** <note>original: *album firmatum*; a stable, purified state of the matter</note> within it, even if you had sold your whole kingdom and all your men, you would not have paid enough for this work."

**Rhazes** <note>original: *Rasis*; Abu Bakr al-Razi, a famous Persian physician and alchemist</note> in his *Book of Lights* speaks concerning the **governance** <note>original: *regimine*; the management of heat and timing</note> of the work. Thus finally it is fitting to **roast and water** it alternately, so that it neither flees the fire nor emits smoke. And a little later he delivers the governance in this way, saying the **inhumation** <note>the "burial" or placement of the substance in a moist, earthy environment</note> is completed in 21 continuous days; wherefore even if one spends 21 days, it will be necessary to expend the same both before and after. As **Rhazes** says in the *Book of Lights*: It seems extremely necessary for the craftsman to examine, estimate, and love, nor in these things [to allow] anything...

# THE COUNSEL OF MARRIAGE
<note>original title: *Consilium Conjugii*; a famous alchemical text concerning the 'marriage' or union of chemical principles.</note>

...you will not be loath to pay the expense. But he who lacks these three things <note>likely referring to the knowledge of authorities, the necessary time, and the financial means to support the work</note> will not deserve to be enriched by the "books of books." Whence through my investigations, I have learned that the completion of the work is achieved in two ways: 

<margin>
The Work is done in two ways: 1. In a glass vessel without the crushing of hands.
</margin>

The first way is in a glass vessel without the crushing of hands <note>original: *sine manuum contritione*; this implies a purely chemical or "natural" process where the substances react on their own through heat, rather than being physically ground or manipulated by the chemist</note>; just as in the first and the last <note>referring to the beginning and end of the Great Work</note>, so also in my own work, it is done by the ministry of **fire** and **water** alone. In this way, it dissolves itself, and so it joins itself; it whitens itself and adorns itself with redness; it makes itself yellow and black; it betroths itself and conceives from itself, until it hastens the end of the work.

For it is said in riddles: the **Stone** kills itself with its own dart <note>original: *iaculo*; a metaphor for the internal spirit or solvent that breaks down the matter</note>, it macerates and betroths itself, it reddens itself, dissolves itself, and congeals itself; it fixes and drinks itself in. And the **soul** prepares the **body**, and the body the soul. 

<margin>
The author's teacher
</margin>

Nor will you see the **whiteness** <note>the *Albedo* stage of purification</note> first, as my instructor told me, until the whole body has been congealed by drinking itself in, neither separating the water from it, nor a third part, nor even half of it; since this arrangement is not made by human hands, as **Morienes** <note>a legendary hermit-alchemist who supposedly taught the art to the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid</note> says. But **fire** and **water** are enough for you, and these hold the record <note>original: *registrum*; likely referring to the governance or regulation of the process</note>, so that...

<meta>catchword: quando (when)</meta>

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process as a self-contained, natural cycle where the substance (the Stone) acts upon itself—dissolving, coloring, and congealing—without physical intervention by the chemist's hands, guided only by fire and water.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 186, which described the alchemical Stone as a "self-performing" entity that acts without human intervention. This page moves into the specific stages of "whiteness" (albedo) and the dialogue between Morienus and King Khalid regarding the "arrangement" or "disposition" of the chemical natures.</meta>

->OF THE SUN AND MOON<-

<margin><unclear>when the whiteness...</unclear> <unclear>degrees of fire...</unclear> <unclear>Morienus</unclear> <unclear>King Khalid</unclear></margin>
When the whiteness <term>albedo; the second major stage of the alchemical Great Work, signifying purification</term> appears, they proceed in six degrees of fire, as described by the **Philosophers**, and those who follow them prove this path. First, **Morienus** <note>Morienus the Hermit, a legendary 7th-century figure credited with teaching alchemy to the Arabs</note> said to **King Khalid** <note>original: *Rex Kalippe*; refers to Khalid ibn Yazid, an Umayyad prince and early patron of alchemy</note>: "Since this arrangement is not made by human hands, what is this arrangement by which the whole mastery <note>original: *magisterium*; the Great Work or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note> is perfected?" The Master responds to him, that this arrangement is the transformation of natures—of the hot with the cold, and the moist with the dry—a marvelous connection. Concerning this, the wise man said:
<margin>♃ ☿ ☉ <note>Alchemical symbols for Tin (Jupiter), Quicksilver (Mercury), and Gold (the Sun)</note></margin>
that ***Azoth*** <note>the universal solvent or the "mercury of the philosophers"</note> *and fire* wash the **latten** <note>original: *latonem*; an impure base metal alloy representing the matter in its unrefined, dark state</note> and purify it, and its darkness is thoroughly taken away from it. For if you have correctly arranged the measure of the fire, Azoth and fire are sufficient for you in this arrangement for the whitening of the latten and its perfection. And this arrangement is the extraction of water from the earth, and the release of that same water upon the earth, until the earth itself rots <note>original: *computrescat*; the stage of "putrefaction" where the matter breaks down to be reborn</note>. And this water putrefies with the earth and is cleansed; once cleansed, the whole mastery is directed by God’s help. For then it lacks stench, and darkness, and all moisture; it is white, brilliant, and capable of receiving the **tincture** <note>the soul or coloring agent that transforms the metal into its final state</note>. Therefore, first extract the oil from the earth, and dissolve in it the binding and coagulating medicine—that is, the earth—and **sublimate** <term>to heat a substance until it turns to vapor and then becomes solid again upon cooling, used for purification</term> it again through the **alembic** <term>a specialized glass vessel with a "beak" used for distillation</term>, and calcine it; the expressed calcination remains; then dissolve, distill, coagulate, and wax it with its own oil, and cast that *Xir* upon the fermented Mercury. 
<margin>Xir becomes Ixir for the Elixir</margin>
That *Xir* is then an *Ixir* suitable for making the Elixir. And know for certain that the body is thus dissolved by means of the saltiness <note>original: *salsedine*; likely referring to the "Salt of the Wise," a necessary chemical agent in the dissolution of the "body" or metal</note>...

...you, by reason of the metallic humidity, which—unless it enters into its own body from which it is extracted—[will not perform its work], etc. Later, he says the same thing: Let **Mercury** be taken and placed into custody between the **burning** and the **burnt**, and let it be committed to the **Sun** and **Moon** <note>referring to gold and silver, or the qualities of heat and cold</note>, which are the sources of cold and heat; and let our earth bring forth the **sprout of whiteness** and of **redness**, fixed and fusible. If the result is **penetrable** <note>meaning the medicine is able to sink into and transform base metals</note>, then you have reached the end; and on this point, all [the authorities] seem to agree.

Nor shall I explain it, except for the more difficult parts of the book, by applying the testimonies of various Philosophers or their own explanations; nor can I discover anything new beyond the sayings of the Philosophers...

<header>COUNCIL OF MARRIAGE</header>

<margin>Francis I</margin>
...of the Philosophers, because there is one thing, and its way is one. And this is the second part of the treatise of the book concerning the mass of the Sun and Moon—that is, the Council of Marriage <note>original: *Consilium Conjugii*; a famous alchemical title referring to the "marriage" or union of chemical opposites</note>—regarding the demonstration of the *Book of the Three Words*. The Philosophers say, therefore, that the combinations of two contraries—which are cold and moist, being watery and combustible <note>original: *adustiua*; likely meaning "capable of being scorched" or volatile in this context</note>—are not friendly to heat and dryness. This is because heat and dryness destroy the cold and moist through their dominant power; then that spirit is turned into a most noble body. It does not flee from the fire, but flows like oil in the fire, and it is a most beautiful, everlasting tincture <term>original: *tinctura*; in alchemy, a potent substance or "elixir" capable of transforming and "coloring" base metals into gold or silver</term>.

Wherefore it is necessary to know how we may manifest its hidden part—that is, the hot and dry—and hide its manifest parts, namely the cold and moist. This hidden part is of the nature of the Sun <note>original: *Solis*; referring here to Gold, the solar metal</note>, the most worthy and precious of all hidden things. It is a single tincture—that is, singular, and there is no other—because it is the permanent water which lives forever, and the vinegar of the Philosophers. Hence Mundus <note>a philosopher frequently cited in early alchemical literature</note> says in the *Assembly* <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers"</note>: "Our gum <note>original: *gumma*; used here to describe the thick, resinous state of the alchemical matter</note> is not improved, except by gold alone." I say that

<image-desc>A small hand-drawn flourish or sigil in the bottom left margin, consisting of a vertical stroke with two horizontal cross-marks.</image-desc>

<meta>catchword: gum <note>original: *gumma*</note></meta>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical "marriage" of opposites, where heat and dryness transform a cold, moist "spirit" into a stable, gold-like "tincture." It emphasizes that the goal is to reveal the hidden "solar" nature of the matter using "permanent water" or "philosophical vinegar."</summary>

<keywords>Council of Marriage, Sun and Moon, four qualities, tincture, permanent water, vinegar of the philosophers, Turba Philosophorum, Gold</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Council of Marriage," a section within the "Book of the Three Words." It follows a discussion on the "permanent water" and the "vinegar of the philosophers," moving now to describe the superior nature of the "gum" used in the alchemical work.</meta>

<page-num>197</page-num>
<header>OF THE SUN AND MOON.</header>

Our gum is stronger than gold; those who know it hold it to be more precious than gold. Indeed, we honor gold with 
<margin><unclear>gold</unclear></margin>our gum <note>original: *gumma*; in alchemy, this refers to a viscous or resinous substance produced during the chemical process, often considered the "glue" that binds the elements or the "mercury of the wise"</note>, because without it, gold is not improved. And thus it is that the Philosophers, when writing about it, tell nothing clearly. For if common 
<margin><image-desc>pointing hand</image-desc></margin>merchants knew of it, they would not sell it at such a cheap price. And Arsuberes <note>an ancient authority often cited in alchemical florilegia</note> says: "Know that there is one thing stronger and more sublime than all natures among the Philosophers, yet among the foolish it is more vile; but we 
<margin><image-desc>pointing hand</image-desc></margin>venerate this thing." And if Kings recognized this, they would not allow it to come to the poor. Oh, how wonderfully the nature of this gum turns gold into spirit, and in what a marvelous way it excels and surpasses all things! And Pandophilus <note>a recurring figure in alchemical dialogues like the *Turba Philosophorum*</note> says: "I say that no body is more worthy and pure than the Sun <note>referring to Gold</note>, without which and its shadow—that is, the Moon <note>referring to Silver</note>, which of itself is dark—no tincturing venom <note>original: *uenenum tingens*; in this context, 'venom' refers to a potent, transformative liquid or elixir that 'colors' or changes the nature of metals</note> is generated. But he who strives to compose the venom of the Philosophers without these, is entirely mistaken; however, he who knows how to tincture with the venom together with the Sun and its shadow, comes to the secret <note>original: *archanum*</note>, which is called the

->## OF THE SUN AND MOON<-

namely, that they are of a similar nature and that both are of a most tempered and strongest composition. These are the three words of premeditated intention, and they are symbolic words, after the likeness of the **fetus of the Philosophers** <term>Fetus of the Philosophers: a common alchemical metaphor where the creation of the Philosopher’s Stone is compared to the nine-month gestation of a human child</term>. 

<margin>Three WORDS <br/> <unclear>S. A. G.</unclear> <br/> <unclear>...</unclear></margin>
*Water* preserves the fetus in the womb for three months; *Air* cherishes it for three months. *Fire* guards it for just as many [three months], which is to say, when those same number of months have been completed, the blood—which was cherished in the navel and by which the infant was nourished—ascends to the maternal breasts of the woman. And there, after the difficulties of birth, it takes on the **whiteness of snow** <note>original: *niuis candorem*; this refers to the transformation of blood into breast milk, but alchemically symbolizes the "Albedo" or whitening stage of the work</note>. To the infant, indeed, the exit will never be open until he draws in all the airy breaths. Having finally come out, he opens his mouth and is suckled. 

And from these words we must, with sharp wit, extract the **two and the seven** <note>likely referring to the two principles (Sulfur and Mercury) and the seven planetary metals or seven stages of the process</note>, which are understood from these words. And **Senior** <note>Zadith Senior (Ibn Umayl), a famous 10th-century Arabic alchemist whose "Chemical Tables" were widely translated</note>, by the authority of **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the alchemical tradition</note>, says the same thing in this way: 

"And know that water serves it in the belly for the first three months. Then for three months air, then fire for the following three, and it cooks it and perfects it. And when the nine months have been completed, [the blood] is cut..."

For all things were made from water. The father of this water is the **Sun**; its mother is the **Moon**, as says **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy; this phrase echoes the "Emerald Tablet," a foundational alchemical text</note>, the father of philosophers. And for certain, the Sun is the principle of the generation of man in the **oblique circle** <note>original: *obliquo circulo*; referring to the Ecliptic or the Zodiac, whose tilted path governs the seasons and the cycles of life</note>. 

Wherefore, philosophers speaking of this art teach in different ways: some teach naturally, as regarding male and female, the moist and the hot, and other natural principles. Others explain it arithmetically, speaking of the nine and the tenth part, and how two [and] three make six, or one and two make three <note>Alchemical numerology often uses these ratios to describe the proportions of elements or the stages of the transformation process</note>. Others teach astronomically, as regarding the **Sun, Moon, and Planets**, whose light and motion are the cause of these things; and they are everywhere and always present in power, and in any [subject] thing...

And if there were no moisture in the womb, the seed would not be dissolved, nor would the fetus be procreated. Truly God established that blood for the nourishing of the seed until it brings forth the fetus; and when it has been brought forth, it is nourished only by milk, as long as the little one...

<page-num>207</page-num>

<meta>catchword: But</meta>

...it is, and the more it is burned by **natural heat** <term>calor naturalis: The internal, vital heat inherent in matter, often distinguished from the external "accidental" heat of the furnace</term>, the more it is strengthened in its bones, led into youth, and when it comes to old age, it is sufficient for itself. In a similar way, it behooves us to labor in this **work** <term>opus: The "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term>, knowing that <margin>Nothing is generated without heat.</margin> nothing is generated without heat. And know that the intense heat of a **bath** <term>balnei: Referring to the *balneum Mariae* or water-bath, used to provide gentle, even heat to the glass vessel</term> causes exhalation, but cold indeed causes the work to perish. If, however, the heat be temperate, then it is pleasant to the matter, and thus the veins are eased, and the heat is totally increased. 

And that **Philosopher** <note>Likely referring to Pythagoras or Bonellus from the preceding debate in the *Turba Philosophorum*</note> most beautifully teaches the preparation, generation, and nutrition of the **fetus**. And **Aristotle** <note>A central authority in medieval alchemy; many texts like the *Secretum Secretorum* were attributed to him to grant them legitimacy</note> says in the *Letter to Alexander*: "And know that no one born is born except from a father and a mother." 

For the infant, when it exhausts all the spirit of its matter required for its organization, is born naturally, without its [previous] nourishment. And just as in nine months the infant exhausts all airy breaths, and the **menstrual blood** <note>original: *menstruum*; in alchemy, the "feminine" or "mercurial" liquid that nourishes the "masculine" seed in the vessel</note> is converted into a milky form, so in nine months the first work is completed—namely the **second whiteness** <term>albedo: The stage of the work where the matter reaches a brilliant white state, signifying it can now turn base metals into silver</term>—because the whole work is **coagulated** <note>original: *coagulatur*; the process where a liquid substance becomes solid or fixed</note>; yet it is dissolved around six months, according to the author’s experience.

<margin>Mercury is dissolved in 21 days and coagulated in 21</margin>
...**Philosophical [Mercury]**. And thus the extracted **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius* ☿</note> is taken; it is red in its hidden nature, but in its manifest state it is white, and perhaps on the 21st day it is dissolved, and it is coagulated in just as many days. 

But note the **rule of the fire** <term>regimen: the controlled management of heat intensity throughout the alchemical process</term> in the extraction of this Mercury, according to what **Avicenna** <note>original: *Auicenna*, referring to the Persian polymath Ibn Sina, whose views on the formation of metals were central to medieval alchemy</note> says: That heat in a moist body first produces **blackness** <term>nigredinem: the "nigredo" stage, representing the death or putrefaction of the matter, which must occur before it can be purified</term>. Then it strips it of blackness, because it entirely exhausts its moisture, and when that moisture overflows, the heat whitens it, as is seen in the heat used by common folk. 

For first in the work all things are blackened, secondly they are whitened, and thirdly, through a greater intensity of fire, the whole composition must be reduced to ash. So also in our work, from the abundant moisture in the body, blackness is generated in the fire. <margin>Ethel</margin> This blackness, moreover, is called **Ethel from Ethel** <note>original: *Ethel de Ethel*. A term of Arabic origin often used in Latin alchemy to refer to the "spirit" or the "blackest" essence of mercury</note>, that is, **quicksilver** from quicksilver.

For this dissolution of blackness is the **moisture of the Moon** <note>Referring to the lunar/silver principle</note> and the **fatness of the Sun** <note>Referring to the solar/gold principle</note>, to which nothing is more kin...

the solar spirit,
the magnesia <term>Magnesia: In alchemical texts, this does not refer to the modern mineral but to a mystical "primitive earth" or a base substance capable of being transformed.</term>
of the two
luminaries. <note>The Sun and the Moon</note>
</margin>
I will demonstrate. Therefore, sulfur is fire, namely the Solar splendor, and the powerful spirit of magnesia is earth—that is, the dead body of the two luminaries—which occupies a different place than the body of water. For each is placed separately, namely the body and the water; and the original water floating on top is **Mercury**, which is called the primary water. The second is air, that is, the vapor of the body.

<margin>
$\mercury$ <note>Symbol for Mercury</note>
</margin>

And **quicksilver** <note>original: *argentum uiuum*</note> is the primary water, flowing through the whole body. But the spirit is the secondary water, in which the lunar humor and the solar fatness <note>In alchemy, "fatness" or "oiliness" refers to the combustible, soul-like principle or "sulfur" within a substance that allows it to catch fire or transform.</note> are joined to **Mercury** by reason of their metallic humidity. It follows because all things are nourished, and every sprout is invigorated, and every light ascends, as does every fruit.

<margin>
Simple,
common
**Mercury**
is not of
our work, but
the composite
[is].
</margin>

Indeed, simple, common, and vulgar **Mercury** is not for our work; rather, it is the composite **Mercury** which is suddenly congealed and fixed like that other. But simple Mercury lacks ferment and is not useful to us, and this is a great secret. Whence concerning this composite **Mercury**, **Eximerus** <note>A sage or character appearing in the *Turba Philosophorum*</note> says in the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba Philosophorum* or "The Assembly of the Philosophers"</note>: "I therefore advise you that quicksilver, which [is made] from many things..."

<meta>catchword: ...things [rebus]</meta>

<summary>This page defines the alchemical relationship between sulfur, fire, and Mercury, emphasizing that the "vulgar" mercury found in shops is useless compared to the "composite" Mercury of the philosophers. It describes the interaction between the "solar fatness" and "lunar humor" as the driving forces of metallic growth and transformation.</summary>
